**Retrieval/planner/crop/artifact connection implemented locally — 2026-10-21,
Critical / Must have, existing Packages 1/3/6:** ProtectedCampaignArtifact now
continues the protected retrieved-planner action with the separately saved crop,

GCS artifact storage and immutable BigQuery artifact/scoring records. The trusted
continuation retains exact source context only within the bounded worker action
and clears IAP headers/context on success or failure. No extra marketer stage
buttons are introduced. Existing retrieval/embedding records are recovered rather
than rerun. Native read IDs/times remain evidence, not new logical slot identities.
Fresh product reads are used for rendering; original source/crop/registry/access
bindings are rechecked around storage, scoring and recovery.

Measured structural scoring recomputes dimensions, exact planned copy/geometry,
font hashes, lineage and actual scaled product pixels (MSE), and records numeric
measurements. It does not infer visual quality, OCR copy, legal/brand safety or full
47-check qualification. The compositor is deterministic; source-grounded generative
rendering remains open. Cold recovery performs no render/upload/reserve/publication.
Unknown original slots cannot remint or reupload; lost committed responses reuse
original jobs. A source change after upload holds the uncertain record and preserves
the object, rather than fabricating a success or repairing data.

**Single-product campaign integration — local only, 2026-10-21,
Critical / Must have, existing Packages 1–3:** the user directs the next acceptance
target to one complete Rejuvenate campaign using the already saved material.
`campaign_retrieved_planner.py` joins a recovered semantic evaluation to the exact
registered private planner input. It binds corpus, principal, full scope, policy,
image manifest and bytes, ordered chunk text/provenance and original measurement/
rerank hashes. Gold-label rationales are excluded from planner context; source text
remains unapproved reference material. Changed input does not remint an existing
planner slot; use a separately pinned new campaign run, preserving the old pilot.

`campaign_retrieved_planner_runtime.py` composes existing protected retrieval,
source and planner services into one private operation. It recovers baseline
retrieval only, then runs or recovers semantic reranking and planning with fresh
source guards and exact original records. Default execution is disabled. Current
registered identity-only options retain product name plus View product; no claim
permission is inferred from ranking. Missing or uncertain records cannot trigger
redispatch on Recover. Request context is cleared on success and failure.

**Marketing workflow and background preparation boundary — 2026-10-21,
Critical / Must have within existing Packages 1–3:** the marketing user should
select a product, describe a campaign and receive a grounded, checked banner or
an actionable missing-evidence message. The current corpus, embedding, retrieval
and recovery buttons are private engineering controls; requiring a marketer to
click each stage is a product gap, not the target MVP interaction. The existing
six-package queue and approval gates remain controlling.

Source intake should trigger a durable background preparation sequence: preserve
original bytes and generation in GCS, classify the asset by its actual content,
extract document text and image observations with provenance, bind the SKU/scope,
create version-pinned embeddings, store vector records and index manifests in
BigQuery, then publish current per-SKU readiness only after exact source checks.
Use the existing source/transaction/job foundations and original-job recovery;
do not treat an upload, filename or brand-site origin as claim approval. Recheck
changed/revoked source and permission state before retrieval and use. A failed or
uncertain stage remains pending or needs review, with no duplicate provider call.
Background preparation must process supporting images as references; the two
JPEGs inspected in this pilot are ingredient/benefit and clinician-review images,
not clean product pack shots. The selected WEBP includes a clinical-results badge,
so image fidelity and claim applicability remain open.

At request time, the protected campaign path resolves the selected product's
current ready corpus, embeds the new query as needed, filters by eligibility,
retrieves/reranks relevant evidence, checks the exact prompt and claim authority,
generates the concept and source-grounded artifact, scores it and stores a draft.
The user sees progress and the needed business clarification, not pipeline
operations. Query embedding, retrieval, reranking, model generation and artifact
checks are request-dependent work; they cannot all be precomputed offline. A
background-ready corpus never itself grants campaign generation or release.

Current evidence proves one durable three-chunk corpus, its text embeddings and
index manifest, and a source-reviewed retrieval calibration with recovery. It does
not prove a general background scheduler, automatic classification, image
embedding, clean image/claim authority, semantic reranking, an artifact or an
end-to-end marketer request. The local private rerank/source-use prototypes are
not deployed. Package 2 must connect incremental preparation and readiness to
the existing protected path; Package 3 must finish applicable rendering/quality
checks. Full qualifying pipeline benchmark remains **0/159**.

**Interrupted bulk-upload recovery fixed/deployed — 2026-10-19
(Critical / Must have, existing Package 1 intake/recovery):**
The seven-image batch stopped after its three saved-prefix files when the page
refreshed/disconnected. Code inspection established the design flaw: the remaining
browser-owned loop/file objects had no durable pending-work manifest. Two small
implementation batches factor the unchanged per-file canonical record preparation,
add protected durable batch intents and connect server/UI progress/resume controls.
All originals are size/type/category validated and generation/hash-pinned in private
GCS before the BigQuery batch intent is published. The same existing submission
table stores batch_v1_ content-addressed records with batch_scope (not per-file scope),
so normal submission history/extraction stays isolated. No table or IAM additions.
Every individual save retains its original submission ID/global native job; state
checks exact stored rows and original job SQL/config/outcome before skipping saved
files. Reconnect discovers the batch and resumes pending files from staged originals
without relying on browser memory. Missing jobs, changed bytes/context/access or
uncertain responses stop safely. No background authorization cache or blind retries.

The UI stages the selection in one protected action, then advances one pending file
per rerun and offers Resume/Pause plus saved/pending progress. Full-page reruns are
used at completion so both initial/full renders and fragment actions are valid.
New-batch focus cannot be lost behind an older completed selector. Individual limits
remain 5 MiB; batches are bounded to 100 entries/64 KiB manifests. This is recoverable
per-file processing, not all-or-nothing receipt publication. A crash before the batch
intent commits can still require reselection; inspect durable batches first.

Sixty focused tests, compilation and dependency checks pass, including seven-file

## Active delivery contract: workable MVP first — 2026-10-16

This is the controlling delivery order, authorized by the user's MVP-first direction.
Historical checkpoints below describe evidence at their dates, not the current
execution queue. Preserve priority/milestone IDs and existing implementation; do
not rebuild completed storage, registry or execution foundations. The work packages
below group existing scope and are not new priorities. All remain OPEN until their
end-to-end exit evidence is recorded. Documentation is not implementation evidence.

### MVP product boundary

Deliver one protected marketing chatbot that takes a campaign request, clarifies
missing information, retrieves and reranks current evidence/references, produces a
grounded brief and original-product-image banner, validates it before release,
performs bounded correction when appropriate, and supports download and feedback.
BigQuery remains the record/vector authority; GCS retains original/artifact bytes.
Include hybrid lexical/vector retrieval plus reranking, source-image-grounded
generative rendering and multiple validated output formats/markets in MVP. Reuse
the existing renderer as the initial integration path and an explicitly labeled
composition option; it does not satisfy the generative-rendering exit criterion.
Start with one evaluated server-configured model profile per stage/image task;
user model selection is Phase 2. Scope product/channel/market support explicitly
from current authoritative evidence; no silent expansion to unsupported N&T claim
approvals or unreviewed markets. Synthetic analytics remain labeled simulations.

An allowed personal Google account through IAP is sufficient for the limited MVP
pilot. Existing application membership/ownership checks remain mandatory. Google
Workspace domain/groups and broader multi-team onboarding stay Phase 2. Do not
claim a team-ready deployment from the personal-account pilot.

Version it, preserve eligibility and required exact facts, verify no-match behavior and compare labeled relevance/ordering with the cosine baseline plus latency/usage. | 3 | P6/P15: E1/E2/E4/E5, G1/G2/G4, X1 | Implement original-image-grounded generative rendering, validated format variants and market/channel-specific authority. Enforce stage-appropriate evidence, approved-copy, brand-memory, schema, safety and rendered-banner checks before releasing any enabled response/artifact. Clarify for genuine missing information; unavailable reads cannot masquerade as absent evidence. Applicable required failed/unavailable/inconclusive checks withhold output. Execute the reviewed benchmark and real pipeline regressions; no activation before applicable evaluation gates pass. | Critical / Must have | Original-image composition and partial checkers exist; generative rendering, format/market acceptance and complete runtime gates pending |
| 4 | P6/P9: E2, U4/U5, G/X contracts | Implement an initial default of at most one quality-correction attempt, configurable only inside server limits and a total token/deadline budget. Retry only repairable, known outcomes; never retry authorization, missing approval/evidence or uncertain provider dispatch. Recheck fresh evidence and all release gates. Persist validation reasons, each attempt, automated feedback and human feedback tied to exact output/prompt/evidence versions. Stop clearly when exhausted. | High / Must have; retry/resource gates Critical | Quality repair loop and user feedback capture pending |
| 5 | P7/P11/P9: O/C, U4/U6 | Complete correlated RAG/model/check/retry telemetry, provider-reported tokens (unknown stays unknown), context accounting/optimization, basic per-user request/token limits and safe failure visibility. Verify effective chat tasks, accessible responsive controls, useful clarification, progress, evidence, download and feedback. Start these records and UI checks in package 1; this package closes their coverage rather than introducing them late. | High / Must have; access/usage limits Critical | Logs/usage/UI foundations exist; comprehensive connected coverage and measured usability pending |
| 6 | P6/P12: E4/E5, A4/A5/U6 | Run the combined hybrid retrieval/reranking, generated-image fidelity, per-format/per-market quality, usability, access/revocation, idempotency, retry and usage-limit acceptance flow. Deploy an immutable image to the bounded IAP pilot, confirm operational settings and rollback, and demonstrate request-to-download plus feedback. Release only after required gates and approvals pass. | Critical / Must have | Not complete |

Packages overlap only where dependencies allow; one primary end-to-end acceptance
goal is active at a time. Registering DRAFTs is not activation. Quality gates must
pass before serving even though the technical path is assembled earlier behind
disabled defaults. A UI-visible draft still needs applicable release checks and
must clearly state its human-review status; preview is not business approval.

### MVP definition of done

- A signed-in permitted user completes request → clarification when needed →
  grounded brief/banner → evidence inspection → download → feedback in the deployed
  pilot, with meaningful progress and actionable failures.
- Required exact prompt versions are registered, evaluated and eligible/ACTIVE
  under BigQuery authority; an authorized operator can block or roll back them.
  No expanded admin dashboard is required for this capability.
- Hybrid retrieval, reranking, source-grounded generative rendering, each enabled
  format/market and UI effectiveness have measured acceptance evidence. Agree numeric
  targets from the baseline before acceptance; unit tests alone are insufficient.
- Every enabled response/artifact satisfies applicable reviewed acceptance rules;
  missing or unavailable required checks block release. Existing 47-rule/159-case
  materials remain the review baseline. Any applicability/scope change needs an
  explicit recorded decision, not deletion of failing checks to complete the MVP.
- Context limits, usage limits and at most one bounded correction operate as tested;
  every invocation/attempt and outcome links to available usage, sources, validation
  and feedback. Never interpret unknown tokens as zero or retry an uncertain call.
- Automated tests and representative live cases verify failures, revoked access,
  duplicate clicks/reconnect, incorrect product/claim, no relevant reference,
  exhausted correction, feedback persistence and output confidentiality.
- Submitted raw evidence/chat text follows the agreed 30-day retention; logs avoid
  unapproved raw-content capture. Submitted evidence remains unverified until
  independently authorized review; absent scientific/commercial authority holds use.

### Expanded MVP acceptance — user instruction, 2026-10-16

The user explicitly brings hybrid-search expansion, generative image rendering and
wider formats/markets into MVP. This supersedes earlier Phase 2 classifications of
these capabilities throughout historical checkpoints. Existing six work packages
and priority IDs remain; scope grows within packages 2/3/5/6. These are planned
requirements, not implemented features or authorization to use unapproved claims.

| Capability | MVP implementation and acceptance | Category |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Hybrid retrieval | Combine bounded lexical and vector creative-reference results with versioned fusion, deduplication and reranking. Apply source/SKU/access/eligibility filters before selection and recheck afterward. Benchmark vector-only, hybrid and hybrid-plus-reranking against labeled relevance/ordering, latency and usage. Required facts/claims remain authoritative exact reads. | High / Must have |
| Generative rendering | Use verified original product images to ground an evaluated image model. Preserve packaging, label, product shape/color and approved copy; prefer controlled product/copy compositing over allowing the image model to redraw labels. Verify final bytes against original evidence and prohibit AI people/before-after imagery. Pin model/settings/assets and record available image usage/cost per attempt. Do not call composition-only output generative output. | High / Must have; fidelity/safety gates Critical |
| Wider formats | Support an explicit versioned format matrix, including dimensions, aspect ratio, safe areas, product legibility and text-fit rules. Proposed initial matrix: existing 1200 × 628 landscape, 1080 × 1080 square and 1080 × 1920 vertical. These proposed dimensions require channel-specification verification before implementation depends on them; no platform-compliance claim is implied. Validate/render/test every supported format, expose choices in chat and persist effective format. | High / Must have; release validation Critical |
| Wider markets | Support an explicit country/channel/locale matrix with current approved wording, warnings, commercial terms and applicable restrictions. Proposed starting countries reuse US/CA/GB/AU benchmark scope; final enabled combinations need evidence/review. Benchmark presence does not grant market approval. Persist destination, ask when missing, apply current overlays and block unsupported/unapproved combinations. Wider markets do not implicitly require multilingual output; supported locales must be explicit. | High / Must have; market/claim authority Critical |

Resolve and record the initial format/market support matrix before implementing
its adapters and fixtures. Numeric quality/latency/cost thresholds require baseline
measurement and agreement. Do not promise arbitrary formats or global coverage.
Extend the reviewed evaluation cases for new image/format/market behavior and obtain
any necessary review rather than treating the old baseline as approval of new scope.
Missing external market authority is an explicit release dependency; software cannot
synthesize it. A blocked country may remain disabled, but the wider-market MVP
criterion cannot be marked complete without the agreed multi-market coverage.

### Phase 2 — retained backlog, not MVP blockers

- **Architecture/library consolidation — user instruction, 2026-10-20
  (High / Good to have; migration safeguards Critical / Must have):**
  [Code review and adoption plan](PHASE_2_STANDARD_LIBRARY_ARCHITECTURE.md).
  Assess Pydantic, Starlette/AnyIO, OpenTelemetry, bounded LangGraph orchestration,
  selective LangChain/Google SDK adapters and reproducible dependency tooling.
  Require demonstrated removal of custom plumbing, preserving BigQuery/GCS
  authority, exact native-job recovery and approval boundaries. Phase 2 only:
  no framework migration or new workstream interrupts the fixed MVP queue.
- Expanded admin/reviewer dashboards, delegated reviewer-management automation,
  self-service onboarding and broad multi-team governance. Keep the minimum existing
  trusted operator controls required for MVP approvals, revocation and rollback.
- Company Google Workspace domain/groups, broader team onboarding and additional
  enterprise identity administration; preserve the SSO design.
- User-selectable models/thinking profiles and wider provider/model comparisons.
- Advanced retrieval/reranker variants and automated ranking tuning beyond the
  measured MVP hybrid retrieval/reranking path.
- User-selectable image models and additional format/market expansion beyond the
  explicit MVP support matrix. Generative rendering and the MVP matrix stay in MVP.
- Broad creative refinement/version comparison, automated real-outcome learning,
  advanced analytics, rich dashboarding and Cloud Billing reconciliation. Basic
  human feedback, token tracking, cost-aware limits and RAG telemetry stay in MVP.
- Scale/performance hardening beyond the bounded pilot and expanded certification/
  governance coverage. Existing access, privacy, evidence, safe-use and output gates
  are not deferred under this heading.

### Working rules until MVP release

**Plan freeze — user instruction, 2026-10-16:** the six-package expanded MVP
contract is fixed. Do not introduce new workstreams between these packages. Work
only on planned MVP deliverables, bugs introduced during that implementation, and
existing bugs that demonstrably impede the MVP workflow. Record each relevant bug
against its affected package and exit criterion. Unrelated bugs/features go to the
Phase 2 backlog without implementation; any future scope change requires explicit
user direction. A discovered dependency is not permission to add a workstream.

Use 2–3 implementation files per reviewable batch, plus separate small test/docs
batches when needed. Reuse existing services and run impacted tests rather than
repeating the whole suite. Every update states the active package/existing ID,
working behavior delivered, actual evidence and the next fixed exit criterion.
Do not introduce extra admin infrastructure or optional abstractions to advance a
package. Record discoveries against an existing exit criterion; genuinely new
features go to Phase 2 unless explicitly reprioritized. Do not treat the remaining
full historical backlog as a prerequisite to a bounded MVP.

versions before activation. Deliver the actual installed request path within this

| Chatbot UI and API | Partial | Local Streamlit chat, interpretation/preferences, synchronous generation and downloads remain implemented. Eleven workflow tables and general writers exist. A default-disabled server-only Streamlit bridge now restores protected history, submits reviewed generation requests, refreshes status, shows safe successful-output summaries and defers verified artifact downloads through prestarted hosts; no serving runtime is installed. Generation workers, shared deployment and reviewer actions remain pending. |
| Banner generation | Implemented as POC composition | Pillow preserves original product imagery. Generative image rendering remains a separate future milestone with additional product-fidelity evaluation. |

The previous full-suite baseline passed 944 tests on 2026-10-10, including 15 isolated-probe tests and 33 sharing executor tests and 31 sharing query-contract tests and 41 sharing coordinator tests, 43 conversation contract tests, 47 membership contract tests, 34 workspace control tests, 43 audit contract tests, 44 evidence contract tests, 45 model-invocation contract tests, 40 attempt/stage-event contract tests, 37 protected-download tests, 28 output/artifact tests, 22 protected-gateway integration tests, 16 real-signature identity tests, 21 access-reader tests, 37 request/job contract tests, 16 resource-setup tests, 15 workflow-schema tests, 21 access-policy tests and the existing chat/UI/model checks. The prior read-only ADC probe verified all eleven deployed schemas in asia-south1, zero workspace/membership/conversation rows and successful summary/full-download job/request/output dry runs (estimated bytes 0 for each). Download tests use real signatures and fake BQ/GCS records to verify byte integrity and current access. The workspace control slice is entirely offline: complete rows, creation/status changes and ACTIVE-only fence proposals are validated, and the access reader uses those row checks. Membership row/creation/change contracts and snapshot-based ADMIN proposal checks are now implemented offline and shared by the reader. They do not authenticate, bootstrap or prove current authorization. Conversation row/creation/sharing contracts and structural archival are now implemented offline and shared by the reader. Creator/owner mutation checks use captured snapshots; archive writes remain disabled pending an explicit lifecycle permission/audit action. The sharing coordinator contract now binds immutable commands, fence/conversation/audit proposals, scoped receipt reuse and unknown commit outcomes offline. It performs no queries, locks or commit verification. The parameterized sharing transaction SQL/query contract now defines access/receipt checks, conditional fence/visibility updates, explicit audit insertion and validated APPLIED/REUSED/UNKNOWN results. It constructs bounded SDK configuration without submitting queries. The disabled-by-default sharing executor now connects signed identity, current access, runtime-sealed original intents, schema preflight, stable native job IDs and correlated recovery. Thirty-three offline tests verify submission/recovery, including real ES256 signature fixtures. The isolated native probe passed 11 fixture cases, including overlapping transactions and rollback/commit classification, and deleted its owned dataset. Read-only authoritative receipt recovery is now implemented and verified with 181 impacted tests and six focused native cases; see [receipt recovery](SHARING_RECEIPT_RECOVERY.md). The separate native retry probe now passes ten fixture cases and 266 affected offline tests with owned cleanup; managed signing-key deployment, management writers and HTTP/UI wiring remain pending; see [native sharing evidence](SHARING_INTEGRATION.md). The audit contract slice is offline; 4.5 now adds fresh bounded access/service audit producers and replay envelopes. It still does not authenticate, prove commits or persist audits; transactional publication remains 4.6. Only isolated synthetic probe resources were written and removed in the earlier native slices; no serving data/schema, uploads, IAM or UI flow changes occurred in 4.5. See [sharing coordinator contract](WORKFLOW_DATA_DESIGN.md#implemented-sharing-coordinator-contract), [conversation contracts](WORKFLOW_DATA_DESIGN.md#implemented-conversation-contracts), [membership contracts](WORKFLOW_DATA_DESIGN.md#implemented-membership-contracts), [workspace contracts](WORKFLOW_DATA_DESIGN.md#implemented-workspace-control-contracts), [audit contracts](WORKFLOW_DATA_DESIGN.md#implemented-audit-contracts), [evidence contracts](WORKFLOW_DATA_DESIGN.md#implemented-evidence-snapshot-contracts), [invocation contracts](WORKFLOW_DATA_DESIGN.md#implemented-model-invocation-contracts), [execution contracts](WORKFLOW_DATA_DESIGN.md#implemented-attempt-and-stage-event-contracts), [download protection](ACCESS_OWNERSHIP_DESIGN.md#protected-artifact-downloads), [output validation](WORKFLOW_DATA_DESIGN.md#implemented-output-and-artifact-contracts) and [setup verification](WORKFLOW_DATA_DESIGN.md#record-boundaries). Five live model-continuity cases previously passed. Formal RAG quality evaluation, deployed SSO, UI enforcement, a live workflow-artifact download, populated-table performance, loader idempotency and full generation-workflow concurrency/recovery remain unverified. Fixture sharing concurrency is verified separately.

Configured models are Gemini 3.5 Flash for request interpretation and campaign planning, gemini-embedding-001 for text vectors, and multimodalembedding@001 for image vectors. Interpretation uses MINIMAL thinking with a 1,024-token output limit; planning uses LOW thinking with a 4,096-token output limit. The model endpoint remains asia-south1. Streamlit is the initial local UI framework. The migration completed before Gemini 2.5 Flash's documented October 20, 2026 retirement; monitor lifecycle changes and evaluate each future replacement. See [Google model lifecycle](https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-agent-platform/models/model-versions) and [the recorded validation](MODEL_CONTINUITY.md).

## Implementation priority order

This is the fixed scope with severity-based scheduling, updated on 2026-10-11. Priority 1, priority 2's foundations and priority 3's read/download foundations are complete; continue with priority 4. Existing product ingestion, synthetic business data, basic RAG, original-image composition and the first local chat slice are retained; do not rebuild them. Priority numbers and milestone IDs identify scope. The delivery lanes below determine execution order when optional work is deferred. A row may complete only part of a milestone; its exit criterion determines that scope.


### MVP reranking and UI effectiveness — user-approved scope, 2026-10-16

Both are **High / Must have** MVP requirements. This supersedes the earlier
recommendation to defer reranking/UI polish for these specific functional scopes;
cosmetic polish remains optional. The subsequent expanded MVP instruction below
promotes hybrid search, generative rendering and the agreed format/market matrix
to High / Must have; it supersedes the earlier narrow scope. This is a plan
change, not delivered functionality or a measured acceptance result. Preserve
existing priority IDs; implement in 2–3-file batches.

- **Reranking — R1/R2/R3, priorities 6/14:** retrieve a bounded eligible candidate
  pool, rerank semantic creative references for campaign relevance, deduplicate,
  select top K and recheck authority. Use request/channel/objective alignment;
  synthetic analytics must retain simulated provenance. Exact product facts,
  warnings and approved wording remain authoritative reads outside this ranking.
  Version the reranker/configuration and record candidate IDs, initial/final ranks,
  scores, method/version, no-match/failure reason, duration and available usage.
  Evaluate precision/recall at K and an ordering metric such as NDCG at K against
  labeled queries and the cosine baseline; record p95 latency and available token/
  cost measurements. Thresholds must be agreed from baseline evidence, not invented.
  Do not claim efficacy from synthetic campaign performance.
- **UI effectiveness — U4/U5/U6 and E1/E2/E4, priorities 6/9/12:** a marketing user
  can submit a request, understand interpreted details, answer relevant evidence-gap
  questions, follow progress, inspect a grounded banner/brief and evidence, provide
  feedback and download permitted output. Show validation/review state and safe
  actionable errors, including retry-limit exhaustion. Preserve drafts across
  supported reruns/reconnects and prevent duplicate submissions. Provide legible
  responsive layouts, keyboard navigation and labeled controls; do not expose
  admin/configuration details as mandatory campaign steps.
- **Acceptance evidence:** test representative marketing tasks end to end, including
  ambiguous requests, missing evidence, failure/recovery and feedback. Record task
  completion, time to first valid draft, clarification burden, error recovery and
  user usefulness feedback. Separate usability study results from AppTest/technical
  tests; agree target thresholds and record human findings before declaring this
  criterion complete. Capture telemetry without unapproved raw-content logging.

**Delivery placement:** connect the existing protected campaign path and required
prompt eligibility first; then implement/evaluate reranking alongside the runtime
quality gates. Develop/test the effective chat experience in that same integration
sequence. Complete bounded correction/feedback and usage/RAG telemetry, then run
combined quality and usability acceptance before enabling the MVP pilot. Advanced
admin workflows remain deferred; exact prompt approval/activation and access/release
checks remain required. No new priority or approval is implied.

### Fixed scope and reporting rules

Baseline established on 2026-10-11: preserve the existing priorities **1–16**, their
IDs and exit criteria. The user's severity/necessity direction determines scheduling
without renumbering or adding milestones. User-approved MVP scope update — 2026-10-16: bounded second-stage reranking
under existing R3/priority 14 is now High / Must have for MVP. Hybrid retrieval and source-grounded generative rendering under existing priority
15 are also MVP requirements per the subsequent user instruction. User-selectable
image models and priority 16 real-outcome learning remain later enhancements. Existing IDs are preserved. The A/U/F/M/P/E/R/O/G/X/C
checklists describe requirements within this sequence, not additional priorities.

- Report the current priority and fixed substep ID, severity, Must have/Good to have
  classification, applicable release, completed deliverable, verification evidence,
  and the next existing ID. Do not introduce a new list
  of milestones in each completion report.
- Implementation tasks and necessary fixes belong under the existing acceptance
  criteria. Splitting their execution across turns does not create another
  milestone or change the exit criterion. Do not renumber completed work.
- Newly discovered work outside these criteria must be described as a proposed
  scope change, with its reason and impact, and agreed with the user before it
  becomes part of the baseline. A blocked dependency remains attached to its
  existing milestone; it does not silently expand the plan.
- Mark a fixed substep complete only when its listed exit criterion is met.
  Distinguish offline contracts from working persistence/runtime integration.
  Preserve deferred company identity setup and existing disabled execution gates.

### Severity, necessity and delivery lanes

Every planned change has two independent tags. Severity describes the impact of
omitting or breaking the capability; it is not an estimate of implementation effort.
Necessity describes whether the capability is required for the named release.

| Severity | Meaning in this project |
| --- | --- |
| Critical | Protects identity/access, approved grounding, immutable evidence/data integrity, or prevention of unsafe/duplicate dispatch. An unresolved failure blocks the affected operation or release. |
| High | Required for reliable workflow completion, measurable quality, operational visibility or bounded spend. Complete before the release relying on it. |
| Medium | Improves flexibility, efficiency or user experience; a bounded alternative already supports the pilot. |
| Low | Presentation or diagnostic convenience with no effect on required access, grounding, recovery or spending controls. |

**Must have** means a release gate for the stated scenario. **Good to have** means
it can be deferred with the affected optional feature disabled. Production-only
Must have work does not block the shared pilot. Controls for an optional feature
become Must have before enabling that feature, regardless of the feature's tag.
No tag changes a completed item's status or weakens its recorded acceptance checks.

Execute dependency-ready **Critical + Must have** first, then **High + Must have**.
A High dependency may precede the Critical operation it enables (for example
4.1 before 4.2). Do not implement Medium/Low convenience work while a ready
Critical/High Must have item remains in the current delivery lane. Blocked company
identity setup stays under its existing ID while independent implementation continues.

| Delivery lane | Existing IDs and selected scope | Scheduling |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Shared-pilot Must have | 4.1–4.7 → 5 → 6 → 7 → 9's protected UI/review → 10's minimum retrieval-quality checks → 11's essential logging/alerts/screening/compute controls → 12 | Active lane. Respect each dependency and complete Critical/High requirements before enabling shared use. Priority 12 activation requires real IAP identity/role configuration; an explicitly allowed Gmail pilot is planned, with company Workspace configuration required for the team release. |
| Good to have for pilot | 8's model/thinking selector; 9's iterative refinement; 10's optional visual reuse/rank display; 11's comprehensive tracing/dashboards | Deferred. Preserve IDs and full exit criteria; leave features disabled or retain the existing bounded behavior. Full parent milestones stay open until deferred parts are delivered. |
| Broader-production Must have | 13; production portions of P4, E4/E5, O2/O3/O4, G3/G4 and C3/C6 | After pilot evidence, before broader production. Managed scoring E3 is optional if agreed quality gates are satisfied by the benchmark and human review. |
| Later enhancements | 14, 15, 16 | Good to have after core delivery and supporting evidence. Controls become mandatory before any enhancement is enabled. |

priority numbers rather than becoming extra Priority 4 exit gates.

The first nine 4.7 batches are [the protected submission/history/read boundaries,
durable chatbot state adapter and server-only UI runtime bridge](WORKFLOW_SUBMISSION_SERVICE.md).
It authenticates IAP headers, reads exact current workspace/membership/conversation
rows, derives stable request/job/intent/audit identities from the verified principal
and retry key, builds the existing 4.6 workflow-start plan and submits it through
the default-disabled writer executor. It reauthenticates and rereads access before
returning only a minimal persistence receipt. Exact plans are retained only for
bounded same-process retries; they are not a serving data fallback. A cold process
must use the upcoming authoritative BigQuery history/reconnect reader and must not
mint or resubmit a replacement plan. All 84 affected offline tests pass. No live
cloud, model, GCS, serving-row, IAM or activation change occurred.

4.5 was delivered in small, reviewable batches of no more than three changed files
per batch. The completed slices cover fresh canonical product, policy/claim,
structured analytics, ranked campaign creative text and pinned product/campaign/
reviewed-generated-image capture/projection, complete planning-bundle assembly,
and fresh access/service audit production with replay checks. Durable writes are complete under 4.6.

4.6 also uses batches of no more than three changed files. Its first eight batches
are complete offline: [the workflow-start and worker-claim transaction foundation](WORKFLOW_START_WRITER.md)
constructs deterministic, bounded, parameterized transactions for a revision-one
request and QUEUED job, followed by a revision-two RUNNING job, attempt one and
the first retrieval-stage event, one complete canonicalized evidence snapshot and
one PLANNED planner invocation derived from the request's pinned profile. Guarded
post-dispatch publication now records terminal/unknown outcomes, nullable usage,
exact Decimal estimates and separate result/accounting audits; UNKNOWN atomically
fences the job and attempt into recovery. Each path rejects partial or conflicting
replay. Successful output publication now consumes the exact successful invocation,
complete evidence snapshot/audit, supplied complete stage history and fresh trusted
artifact-verification observations. It binds every GCS generation/hash/size/MIME pin,
appends the POC output and seven remaining stage events, and atomically completes the
attempt and job with ten separate audits. Conflicting output-per-job, partial event or
audit replay is rejected. Terminal non-success publication now consumes only an
exact recorded `FAILED` or `BLOCKED` invocation and a supplied ordered stage prefix
ending in the same outcome/error. It appends the remaining events and atomically
closes the attempt and job without an output, with separate mutation/event audits.
Late recorded provider failure can close safely; a timeout alone cannot synthesize
failure. The disabled-by-default workflow writer executor now accepts all seven
typed write plans, preflights the exact dataset/table schemas, recovers an existing
deterministic native job before considering submission, and permits one jobs.insert
only after an authoritative `NotFound`. Lost responses reconcile the same job ID;
unavailable lookup, mismatched metadata, malformed results and ambiguous failed-job
children remain `UNKNOWN`. Exact plan-specific result validators gate `COMMITTED`.
All 603 affected offline tests pass. The final isolated probe then passed six native
duplicate/conflict/rollback/post-commit/original-job recovery cases. Only its owned
temporary dataset was written and deleted; no serving row, GCS object or model was
touched. Step 4.6 is complete within this documented scope.

Calibrate quality scoring with human review (managed scoring is an optional method), expand the benchmark and Responsible AI tests, enforce prompt/model/policy promotion and rollback, and reconcile costs with Cloud Billing exports. Resolve privacy/screening coverage requirements and operational acceptance thresholds before broader production use. Real benefits require real approved evidence; actual campaign launch remains separately authorized. | High | Must have | Broader production, after pilot |

Priorities 1–7 are foundations. Priorities 9–11 supply the required user-facing workflow and operating controls; priority 8 and optional components of 9–11 may be deferred for the pilot. Order 12 is the shared-pilot gate; order 13 is the broader-production gate. An optional feature can be deferred only by keeping it disabled, not by bypassing identity, grounding, ownership, cost or audit checks. No elapsed-time estimates are assigned until the scoped designs and deployment prerequisites are verified.

Scheduling pulls the MVP portion forward without renumbering. | High | Must have | MVP hybrid retrieval/reranking; later advanced variants |

Keep original assets and explicit review history. | High; fidelity/safety gates Critical | Must have generative rendering; model selector deferred | MVP evaluated image rendering |

Ingest them with explicit provenance, canonical SKU links and scoped idempotent writes; produce reviewed learning summaries and test their effect on retrieval/generation. Never relabel synthetic POC performance as real evidence. | Medium | Good to have | Later real-outcome learning |

Split each implementation row into design/contract, service/data, UI/integration where applicable, and verification. Complete and record one substep before advancing to the next. The detailed checklists below retain the acceptance criteria and pending status; creating this order does not mark any feature complete.

## Data and grounding requirements

- BigQuery remains the authoritative database, vector store and eligibility source. GCS retains original images and generated artifacts.
- Local JSON exports and embedding checkpoints remain audit/recovery artifacts. Serving must stop when required BigQuery evidence is unavailable.
- Preserve CI-SKU-0001 through CI-SKU-0020 across every domain. Do not recreate products, remap identifiers or delete unrelated rows.
- New loaders use destination schemas explicitly, scoped idempotent writes, ADC and appropriate Decimal values. No service-account keys are committed.
- Synthetic data stays labeled SYNTHETIC_POC. Historical campaign performance in this POC is simulated.
- Naked & Thriving research supplies CSS, colors, typography and visual inspiration only. Product facts, tone authority and benefit claims require their own approved evidence.
- POC identity approval and POC visual review remain distinct from benefit-claim approval and real campaign launch authorization.

## Chatbot delivery milestones

The UI is the active delivery workstream. Each milestone should produce a reviewable result and appropriate verification before the next step. Backend foundations are added alongside it; UI development can continue while broader evaluation and monitoring are completed.

| Step | Deliverable | Completion criteria | Severity | Necessity | Scenario |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| U1 | Campaign request and response contracts | Extend the existing retrieval request with request/session identity, validated preferences, clarification results and a versioned response envelope. Define job states, evidence references, guardrail results, usage/cost summaries and safe errors. Example conversations show a complete request, missing information and unsupported claims. | High | Must have | Local UI / shared pilot |
| U2 | Backend workflow adapter | Reuse existing fresh retrieval, brief and banner modules behind one service interface. Validate inputs, preserve current grounding restrictions, emit stage events and distinguish successful, blocked and failed outcomes. UI reruns or duplicate submissions do not silently repeat expensive work. | Critical | Must have | Local UI / shared pilot |
| U3 | BigQuery request and job persistence | Store conversations, validated requests, job/stage events, outputs and evidence references using explicit schemas and idempotency keys. Include verified owner/workspace identity, visibility and resolved model/policy versions. Store images in GCS and references in BigQuery. Support authorized status lookup and retry recovery; define concurrency behavior. | Critical | Must have | Local UI / shared pilot |
| U4 | Local chat interface | Submit natural-language campaign requests, clarify missing fields, display interpreted preferences, progress, grounded brief, banner and evidence. Clearly display POC provenance, review state and estimated usage/cost. Use a thin UI backed by the workflow service. | High | Must have | Local UI / shared pilot |
| U5 | Refinement and review | Record feedback and produce a new output version grounded in current evidence. Connect explicit reviewer decisions to existing immutable review history and eligibility rules. Do not infer approval from chat acknowledgments. | Critical | Must have | Shared pilot; refinement optional |
| U6 | Shared demo and deployment preparation | Verify authentication, reviewer authorization, safe errors, cost/concurrency controls and completion gates below. Choose hosting and compute settings, then verify deployment configuration. | Critical | Must have | Local UI / shared pilot |

The first local UI slice implements the synchronous portions of U1/U2/U4 and initial event fields from F1. It covers all 20 existing catalog products without regenerating data. U1/F1/U3 schema setup supplies eleven tables and [implemented pure request/job contracts](WORKFLOW_DATA_DESIGN.md#implemented-pure-contracts). No row loaders or durable UI persistence are deployed. U1/F1 remain partial because remaining row validators, service/API wiring and full guardrail/cost records need completion. U3 workflow persistence, U5 and U6 remain pending.

Verification: the live UI loaded 20 BigQuery products without rendering exceptions; a live CI-SKU-0001 request produced a verified 1200 x 628 banner and 47 evidence references. The preview is unregistered and unreviewed. See [UI_GUIDE.md](UI_GUIDE.md) for usage and current limits.

Continue at **Priority 5 — Critical / Must have — prompt library and core guardrails**. Priority 4 is complete within its documented default-disabled implementation and verification scope. Extract versioned interpreter/planner prompts, register immutable versions in Vertex AI Prompt Management, and publish authoritative lifecycle/eligibility records with exact Vertex version pins in BigQuery. Runtime must resolve the BigQuery-selected version, retrieve that exact Vertex version, verify its canonical hash/settings/output contract and fail closed on mismatch or unavailability. Record explicit allow/block/needs-review decisions with safe model-finish handling. Preserve approved-evidence restrictions and distinguish supported evidence explanations from proposed creative rationale. Current personal-account local use continues; company SSO activation/domain/groups, serving bootstrap and deployed HTTP/IAP remain deferred. Offline and owned-process verification does not activate shared generation or prove deployed runtime behavior.

Workspace is the chosen future provider, rather than an active company account today. Keep the following design for later activation; personal-account local development continues in the meantime.

Priority 12 deployment scope, retain two activation paths (**Critical / Must have
before the applicable shared/cloud pilot**): first an explicitly allowed personal

the marketing team. Both use the same IAP verification and BigQuery role/ownership

Gmail access does not require a Google Workspace subscription. Google documents
Cloud Run IAP access for users without an organization through external-user
OAuth configuration; first-time console setup can configure the OAuth client.

and [OAuth setup](https://docs.cloud.google.com/run/docs/securing/identity-aware-proxy-cloud-run#configure_a_custom_oauth_client).

confirm the exact protected audience and Google account access grant, use controlled
bootstrap for an ACTIVE application workspace and explicit initial roles, then

Google Workspace is confirmed by the user. Recommend company SSO through **direct Identity-Aware Proxy (IAP) on Cloud Run**, followed by application authorization. Google recommends this integration; it protects the default service URL as well as other ingress paths and supports IAM access grants to users/groups. A load balancer is unnecessary solely for IAP. Confirm the GCP project's organization relationship to Workspace before choosing the OAuth client configuration. See [Cloud Run IAP configuration](https://docs.cloud.google.com/run/docs/securing/identity-aware-proxy-cloud-run).

| Approach | Assessment for this application |
| --- | --- |
| Cloud Run + direct IAP + Workspace | Recommended for the internal marketing team: managed sign-in/access boundary with application roles enforced behind it. |
| Streamlit native Google OIDC | Alternative for other hosting or a development integration. It supplies identity; application permissions and session lifecycle still need implementation. Avoid adding a second sign-in flow alongside IAP. See [Streamlit authentication](https://docs.streamlit.io/develop/concepts/connections/authentication). |
| External load balancer + IAP | Consider when routing or infrastructure requirements justify the additional components. Configure IAP at one boundary, following Google's supported deployment configuration. |

Planned request flow: Workspace sign-in → IAP access check → verified application principal → role/ownership checks → BigQuery/GCS/Vertex operations through the runtime service identity. Local ADC authenticates cloud client calls today; it does not authenticate a marketing user. Team members should not need individual BigQuery credentials or service-account keys.

Proposed permissions are additive and scoped to an authorized workspace:

| Application role | Allowed actions |
| --- | --- |
| Viewer | Read/download owned or explicitly team-shared drafts/artifacts. Individual-user sharing is outside the first policy. |
| Creator | Generate drafts; refine and share their own drafts. Team sharing permits reading, not peer editing. |
| Reviewer | Record explicit POC creative decisions for accessible drafts. Benefit-claim approval and launch authority require separate permissions. |
| Admin | Manage app membership, roles, model availability and operating limits. Content access/generation/review require additional roles; private ownership restrictions still apply. Benefit approval and launch remain disabled. |

## Search ranking status and next milestones

Existing ranking is implemented in [campaign text SQL](../sql/retrieve_campaign_creatives.sql) and [generated-banner SQL](../sql/retrieve_generated_banners.sql): eligibility/model/snapshot and canonical-SKU filters precede exact BigQuery COSINE_DISTANCE ordering; ascending distance is more similar. Stable ties use chunk_id for campaigns and asset_id for banners. Default top K is three. The readers revalidate evidence and assign ranks; image retrieval also verifies original GCS objects.

The planner subsequently prefers matching channel and objective among retrieved historical candidates. These are selection preferences, not a learned reranker or performance-weighted score. Product facts, ingredients, rules and claims use scoped authoritative readers; required evidence is not discarded by semantic ranking. Generated-banner retrieval is available as an optional backend path and remains to be explicitly integrated into the chat workflow.

The 22 campaign/banner retrieval unit tests passed on 2026-10-09. This verifies implementation behavior, not relevance quality; the current small synthetic campaign corpus is not a production ranking benchmark.

- [ ] (High; Must have — shared-pilot benchmark; display optional) R1: Establish labeled queries and expected evidence through E1/E2; record baseline recall/precision, irrelevant matches, scope filtering and deterministic tie behavior. Expose distance, rank, source IDs, match flags and ranking method/version as evidence explanations.
- [ ] (High; Must have — shared-pilot minimum quality; tuning optional) R2: Calibrate model/task-specific relevance thresholds and no-match behavior. Evaluate larger candidate pools, channel/objective preferences, deduplication and diversity using the benchmark. Preserve approval, provenance, SKU and future workspace/visibility filters before ranking and after selection.
- [ ] (High; Must have — MVP hybrid retrieval and bounded reranking) R3: Implement a bounded versioned second-stage reranker over eligible semantic creative-reference candidates. Compare against the R1/R2 baseline using labeled relevance, ordering quality, latency and token/cost; agree numeric thresholds before acceptance. Preserve SKU/source/access/approval/freshness filters and deterministic ties, recheck eligibility after selection, and never rerank away required authoritative facts/claims. Return no suitable reference when none qualifies. Failure/fallback behavior must be explicit, measured and visible in telemetry. Bounded hybrid lexical/vector retrieval and versioned fusion are also required for MVP; advanced variants stay Phase 2. Synthetic performance is not proof of business effectiveness.

## User-selectable campaign models

Priority 8 is **Medium / Good to have for the shared pilot**, deferred until the Must have lane is delivered. Before enabling it, all model authorization, evaluation, budget and profile-pinning checks are Must have. Add a **Campaign model** selector and an authorized **Thinking level** control to the reviewed generation form, using server-approved combinations with recommended defaults. Display each actual model name and concise measured quality/latency guidance; show cost estimates when pricing/usage support exists. Initially marketing-user selection controls the campaign planner's brief/copy generation. Independent interpreter model/thinking settings are administered through backend profiles. Separate stage configuration is planned; the current application still shares its configured model and fixes interpreter thinking in code. Embeddings retain their existing model/dimension contract, and the banner continues to use Pillow composition. A future image-generation model needs its own selector, capability checks and fidelity evaluation.

### Independent stage profiles and thinking controls

| Setting | Current behavior | Planned behavior |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Planner model | Backend config, shared with interpreter | Independent validated planner model/profile; authorized campaign-form selection. |
| Planner thinking | Backend-configurable, currently LOW | Profile default plus an authorized user override among that model's evaluated levels. |
| Interpreter model | Reads the planner's configured model | Independent validated interpreter model/profile managed by administrators. |
| Interpreter thinking | MINIMAL fixed in code | Backend-configurable using the selected interpreter model's evaluated thinking controls. |

Implement this portion of priority 8 in small substeps:

1. (Medium; Good to have — pilot; activation controls Must have) **M2 profile design:** Define separate interpreter/planner model IDs, endpoints, supported thinking controls, defaults, output limits, deadlines and profile versions. Preserve Gemini 3.5 Flash/MINIMAL for interpretation and Gemini 3.5 Flash/LOW for planning as initial defaults. Evaluate each model/level combination before enabling it; a level supported by one model must not be assumed valid for another. Handle level-based versus budget-based model APIs explicitly.
2. (Medium; Good to have — pilot; activation controls Must have) **M2 profile publication:** Publish authoritative runtime profiles and allowed overrides into BigQuery with existing role/budget policy. Keep repository files as authoring/configuration artifacts. Missing, disabled or incompatible profiles must fail clearly rather than silently changing model or thinking settings. Safety, evidence and approval rules remain mandatory across all profiles.
3. (Medium; Good to have — pilot; activation controls Must have) **M3 service integration:** Resolve each stage independently, replacing the fixed interpreter setting. Pin the resolved profiles for each invocation and record effective model, thinking control/value, token limits, profile/prompt versions and returned model identity. Include generation-affecting profile/override values in idempotency and output-version records; distinguish clarification invocations from the final planning invocation.
4. (Medium; Good to have — pilot; activation controls Must have) **M4 UI and verification:** Add campaign-planner model/thinking controls only for allowed combinations and roles. Keep interpreter settings in administrator configuration initially. Show effective settings in results/history and test independent stage choices, defaults, invalid combinations, permission/budget denial, unavailable profiles and intentional new output versions after settings change.

- [x] (High; Must have — configured workflow) M1: Validate and migrate the configured planner/interpreter replacement while preserving asia-south1. Completed 2026-10-10 with Gemini 3.5 Flash; recorded candidate/lifecycle review, project access, structured interpretation, original-image input, finish/safety handling and bounded generation. See [MODEL_CONTINUITY.md](MODEL_CONTINUITY.md). This establishes the configured replacement only; additional user-selectable models still require the formal benchmark and M2 policy.
- [ ] (Medium; Good to have — pilot; enforcement Must have before selection is enabled) M2: Define a versioned model catalog, independent interpreter/planner profiles and server-side policy: model ID, endpoint, supported stage/thinking controls and defaults, lifecycle status, evaluation result, compatible prompt/schema versions, token/time limits, pricing reference and permitted roles/workspaces. Author profiles in the repository and publish authoritative runtime choices to BigQuery. Reject arbitrary client model IDs, unsupported model/thinking combinations and disabled/unauthorized selections before model calls. Missing model access produces a clear failure; substitutions must be explicit and recorded.
- [ ] (Medium; Good to have — pilot; validated resolution Must have before selection is enabled) M3: Extend the request/job contract and service with independently resolved interpreter/planner profiles and authorized planner overrides. Remove hard-coded interpreter thinking. Persist selected/resolved/returned model identity, effective thinking settings, profile/catalog/prompt/policy versions and per-stage usage. Include generation-affecting settings in idempotency identity; changing models or thinking settings intentionally creates a new output version. Preserve grounding and approval enforcement for every combination.
- [ ] (Medium; Good to have — pilot; controls/verification Must have before selection is enabled) M4: Add the campaign model/thinking controls and effective per-stage metadata to results/history. Test default choices, independent stage profiles, authorized alternatives, tampered IDs, invalid combinations, lifecycle/access failures, schema/safety compatibility, per-model budgets, rerun deduplication and output-version behavior. Expensive model/thinking choices require server-enforced role/budget policy; UI visibility alone is insufficient.

Ship M1/M2 before M3/M4. The initial list can contain only one validated model until a second passes evaluation; do not promise untested quality tiers or provider support.

## Backend completion milestones

Fifteen structural tests pass. Mutation-fencing/recovery/retention protocols remain undeployed; remaining row validators, loaders, SQL concurrency tests and service integration follow separately.
- [x] (High; Must have — shared pilot) U3 dataset/table infrastructure: [ADC resource setup](../src/ingestion/setup_workflow_tables.py) created and verified ci_application and all eleven empty tables in asia-south1. Recursive schema/layout checks, compatible-create races, interrupted setup and the service partition-filter mirror are covered by sixteen tests. Live repeat --apply created nothing. No workspace/membership rows, workflow writes, UI persistence or IAM changes are included.
- [x] (High; Must have — shared pilot) U1/F1/U3 pure request/job contract foundation: [workflow_contract.py](../src/application/workflow_contract.py) checks canonical preferences, scope, independent stage/thinking snapshots, deterministic input/retry hashes, duplicate intents, interpretation outcomes, job transitions and expected revisions/attempts. Thirty-seven tests pass. Renderer version/settings hash are pinned in existing submission JSON; no table migration or cloud row write. Authorization, published model policy, authoritative lookup/dispatch proofs, remaining row validation and transactional commit are still required.

- [ ] (High; Must have — shared pilot) F1: Define common identifiers and schema versions across request, stage, evidence, prompt, policy, evaluation, usage and cost records. Include request_id, session_id, job_id, trace_id, verified principal_id, workspace_id, owner_id, visibility, canonical SKU, per-stage resolved model/profile/catalog versions and effective thinking settings, timestamps, outcome and safe error code. Client session IDs are not authorization identities.
- [x] (High; Must have — shared pilot) Define proposed BigQuery tables and partitioning/retention before loaders: see [WORKFLOW_DATA_DESIGN.md](WORKFLOW_DATA_DESIGN.md). Retention defaults and coordination are proposals to validate before deployment; nothing is expired or deleted. Future writers record actual workflow state and preserve POC provenance on generated business content.
- [ ] (Critical; Must have — shared pilot) Pin evidence snapshot IDs, image hashes/generations, review revisions, model identity, prompt version and policy version to each run.
- [ ] (High; Must have — shared pilot) Separate durable application/audit records in BigQuery from operational telemetry in Cloud Logging, Trace and Monitoring.

### Prompt library

- [ ] (High; Must have — shared pilot) P1: Extract the existing campaign planner instructions into an initial Vertex AI Prompt Management template while preserving current behavior, allowed options and code validation. Subsequent prompt text changes are authored in Vertex AI Studio and create candidate immutable versions; repository code retains contracts, schemas and tests rather than the editable prompt body.
- [ ] (High; Must have — shared pilot) P2: Define required variables, output schema version, model settings, canonical content hash and lifecycle states for each template. An explicit registration/import operation must read an operator-selected exact Vertex version, validate and canonicalize it, compute its hash, compare it with the current active version and store the full canonical template plus authoritative lifecycle/eligibility state in BigQuery together with project, location, prompt ID and exact version ID. Discovery or import never activates a version; ambiguous outcomes cannot become eligible.
- [ ] (High; Must have — enabled workflows; optional templates deferred) P3: Add request interpretation and planning templates for the enabled workflows; refinement and evaluation templates remain deferred until those workflows are enabled. Runtime resolves a specific BigQuery-approved Vertex prompt/version pair by task, retrieves that exact version and verifies canonical content/settings before use. It never selects `latest`, uses unrestricted vector search for instruction authority or falls back to repository/local content when BigQuery or Vertex is unavailable.
- [ ] (High; Must have — broader production) P4: Compare candidate versions using evaluation results, record authorized promotion decisions in BigQuery and support rollback by changing the authoritative exact-version pin. Track BigQuery registry revision, Vertex prompt/version identity and canonical hash in every model invocation. Vertex restore/latest operations do not independently promote a runtime version.

Product facts, claims, rules and analytics remain fresh retrieved inputs. A template cannot create approvals or replace evidence. Vertex AI Prompt Management is the selected authoring environment, managed artifact registry and collaborative Studio surface; BigQuery remains authoritative for runtime eligibility, lifecycle and exact version selection. Prompt authors may create candidate versions in Vertex. A separate registrar may import and validate an exact version into BigQuery but cannot activate it. The serving identity receives read-only exact-version access and cannot create, restore, delete, import or promote prompts. Because prompt bodies are authored outside Git, the registration review must present a canonical diff against the active BigQuery copy and bind evaluation/promotion to that exact hash. A live read-only `asia-south1` preflight on 2026-10-11 succeeded and found zero registered prompts; it did not create or modify resources.

Priority 5's first two-file offline batch is complete. `prompt_contract.py` defines
strict canonical text, variable, generation-config, safety-setting, output-schema
and exact Vertex resource validation. Separate template and snapshot SHA-256 values
support content deduplication while binding lifecycle records to one exact Vertex
project/location/prompt/version. Revisioned `DRAFT`, `REGISTERED`, `EVALUATED`,
`ACTIVE`, `RETIRED` and terminal `BLOCKED` transitions preserve the immutable
snapshot, require evaluation identity before activation and allow an explicitly
recorded rollback from `RETIRED`. These are supplied-state proposals: they do not
read Vertex, authenticate an actor, prove an evaluation, persist BigQuery rows or
enforce one active version. All 12 focused tests and the 128-test affected contract
slice pass; compilation and whitespace checks pass. P1/P2 remain open until an
exact Vertex version is read, registered and authoritatively persisted.

Priority 5's second two-file implementation batch is complete. The read-only
`vertex_prompt_reader.py` adapter accepts only an explicit numeric prompt ID and

with SDK retries disabled, verifies the returned full resource name, and maps only
supported text-only instructions/template content, model identity, required
variables, generation/thinking/tool settings, safety settings and response schema
into `VertexPromptSnapshot`. It rejects `latest`, malformed identities and ambiguous
or lossy response shapes before registration. The installed `agentplatform` 2.4.0
public `prompts.get` cannot select a version; its exact dataset-version read path is
therefore isolated in the transport and covered by a no-list/no-mutation test seam.
All 12 focused tests and the 140-test affected prompt/workflow/invocation/output/
generation slice pass; compilation and whitespace checks pass. No live Vertex,

registry schemas/setup without creating lifecycle rows or activating a prompt.

not a concurrent serving generation endpoint.

The supplied-state promotion gate accepts REGISTERED→EVALUATED,
EVALUATED→ACTIVE and RETIRED→ACTIVE only with a passed, unexpired evaluation,
matching current policy/benchmark, unchanged exact snapshot and historical
REGISTERED anchor. Activation/rollback retain the evaluation identity and reject
evaluation-linked history before result completion. Its result pins the current
registry revision/digest for a future fenced writer. It is not an access grant,
authoritative read, measured quality result or proof of persistence/commit.

The builder now requires an actual typed operator-policy snapshot with complete

The transaction compares scoped authoritative operator-policy head and full history
using counts and bidirectional set equality before both replay and mutation. A
changed, revoked, deleted, duplicated or partial policy visible to that transaction
therefore denies publication. Existing identity/policy/grant/candidate time checks,
global permission-fence CAS, append-only target history and atomic audit remain.
These assertions operate on BigQuery's transaction snapshot. Future controlled
operator-policy writers must acquire the **same permission fence** to serialize
overlapping role revocations with management writes; assertions alone do not prove
cross-writer serialization. No policy writer has been enabled or exercised here.

The fresh reader captures policy, global control, target and action-specific source
grant/permission in **one parameterized native statement**, without separate policy
or source reads. It bounds row/capture size, detects incomplete partition counts,
requires full canonical histories and exact historical revision/hash pins, and
reconciles the exact original native job SQL/configuration before returning data.
Action checks distinguish grant operators, reviewers and review revokers. Absent
targets satisfy creation only with an unpinned revision-one checkpoint. Expired or
revoked grants cannot support new reviews but may support authorized immutable
review revocation through their original historical grant pin. Claim, offer and
evidence reviews bind their exact stored DRAFT content when constructing the plan.
The supplied `VerifiedIdentity`, server settings and checkpoint floors still need
trusted composition; no JWT-authenticating route or durable replica floor store
was added. Read captures and disabled plans confer no live business approval.

**P6 reviewer grant/review publication contract and query — 2026-10-14:**
The three-file group adds
[campaign_review_publication_contract.py](../src/application/campaign_review_publication_contract.py),
[campaign_review_publication_query.py](../src/application/campaign_review_publication_query.py)
and [21 focused transition/query tests](../tests/test_campaign_review_publication_writer.py).
These are pure supplied-capture transitions and disabled transaction plans; no
trusted writer, business approval or serving publication is activated.

ReviewOperatorPolicy requires explicit IAP audience, separately named grant
operators, reviewer whitelist and review revokers, permitted kinds, versioned
identity, validity window and supplied maximum grant/review durations. No actor,
role or duration is defaulted from bootstrap ownership, ADC, email or model output.
The 256-principal and one-year maximum-duration bounds are technical input caps,
not approved brand policy or selected production lifetimes. Policy digests are
bound into commands; authoritative policy storage/publication/read/revocation and
replica freshness remain pending. This type is not an additional configured
BigQuery policy and does not activate the existing 47 DRAFT acceptance rules.

Five transitions are implemented: CREATE/REVISE/REVOKE_GRANT and CREATE/
REVOKE_REVIEW. Grant assignment requires an explicit operator, reviewer whitelist,
audience/kind scope and bounded validity. Audience/identity are immutable across
grant history; revision cannot reuse any historical version/payload to reactivate
old review pins; revocation is terminal. Expired
ACTIVE grants can be renewed/revoked but cannot authorize review creation.
Review creation requires an actor-bound current grant and an exact stored DRAFT
permission content hash, product/formula identity, proposed eligibility window,
server capture time and bounded expiry. The supplied review-evidence hash is a
pin, not verification of a real human action, document, scientific study or actual
claim eligibility. Independent eligible evidence is still required by permission
approval. Review payloads cannot be edited; owner/explicit revoker actions only
append revocation. Explicit cleanup revocation remains possible after expiry,
grant revocation or a whitelist/kind reduction. Policy changes do not silently
rewrite existing grants or approved claims; explicit lifecycle changes are needed.

Plans share the existing singleton control CAS and immutable history with
permission publication. They recheck exact target/source grant/permission rows,
identity/policy/record windows and append target history, current head, control
history and one audit atomically. Exact complete immediate replay is REUSED;
partial/conflicting audit/head/history state denies. Unrelated records are scoped
out. Empty expected snapshots use typed empty temp tables without unused array
parameters. Bounds remain 64 revisions, 64-KiB captures/new records, 256-KiB config,
64 script statements, 128-KiB SQL and the 768-MiB/120-second query profile. No
retention bypass or new retry slot is introduced.

Existing audit schema is reused under distinct REVIEW_CONTROL_* actions with
permission_kind GRANT/REVIEW. For GRANT actions grant_id/grant_sha256 identify the
new affected grant record; for REVIEW actions they identify the pinned source
grant version. Those references are not operator authorization. The complete
operator-policy digest is pinned by the command hash and retained intent; it is
not a separately stored authoritative policy row. Existing permission receipt
readers do not handle this management namespace; dedicated trusted capture,
execution and receipt recovery are required. Transaction-time checks do not prove
end-to-end deadline enforcement or immediate cross-replica policy revocation.

No cloud calls, schema/resource changes, real grant/review rows, approvals,
model calls, IAM/deployment or activation occurred. Earlier native bootstrap
checks were not repeated and do not establish this new SQL's native behavior.

**P6 authoritative bootstrap reader and disabled trusted executor — 2026-10-14:**
The next three-file group adds
[campaign_bootstrap_reader.py](../src/application/campaign_bootstrap_reader.py),
[campaign_bootstrap_executor.py](../src/application/campaign_bootstrap_executor.py)
and [23 runtime guard tests](../tests/test_campaign_bootstrap_runtime.py).
The server-only reader requires the explicit manifest owner/audience, checks all
13 exact schemas and reads one bounded BigQuery snapshot. An aggregate marker
and explicit count for every table prevent missing/truncated results from looking
empty. At most three bounded JSON cells per table expose overflow; counts above
two, missing/duplicate/foreign markers, ignored oversized cells, future timestamps,
more than 64 KiB total and mismatched native SQL/config/job identity deny. Identity
is rechecked after metadata preflight and before capture release. There is no local
fallback, browser-supplied plan or implied reviewer membership.

ProtectedCampaignBootstrap is disabled by default and uses one immutable trusted
manifest. It authenticates IAP headers, reconciles the fixed global original job
before dispatch and permits one retry-disabled INSERT only after authoritative
NotFound, a fresh complete EMPTY capture and a second owner/manifest-window check.
An existing exact genesis with an absent original denies a new mutation. Known
jobs, mismatched competing manifests, uncertain lookup and positive prior rollback
never remint a job. Lost responses/Conflict reconcile the same ID; recovery never
submits a mutation. Current owner identity is required before result release,
while an expired original dispatch window does not prevent read-only original-job
recovery with a fresh owner assertion. Successful native receipts additionally
require a fresh complete EXACT_GENESIS observation. Partial, missing, conflicting
or subsequently advanced storage withholds the receipt; this immediate-genesis
path is not general historical recovery after grant/review publication.
Correlated failed-job COMMIT/ROLLBACK statuses retain no invented APPLIED result.
Operation cancellation propagates without rollback inference or automatic replay.
Schema preflight RPCs are bounded individually; this slice does not establish a
new hard cumulative HTTP/worker cancellation guarantee.

**P6 controlled singleton-bootstrap contract/query — 2026-10-14:**
The three-file implementation group adds
[campaign_bootstrap_contract.py](../src/application/campaign_bootstrap_contract.py),
[campaign_bootstrap_query.py](../src/application/campaign_bootstrap_query.py) and
[14 focused bootstrap tests](../tests/test_campaign_bootstrap.py). This is a pure,
disabled-by-default proposal/query foundation, not an executed bootstrap.

An explicit trusted manifest pins one exact IAP principal, regional audience,
reason and immutable 1–300 second window. Its digest includes project/dataset/
region and the 768-MiB/120-second query profile. Supplied verified identity must
match that owner/audience, be currently valid and cover the remaining manifest
window. A complete frozen supplied snapshot names all 13 tables; missing tables,
noncanonical/oversized rows or truncation cannot imply emptiness. Only all-empty
state or the exact isolated genesis is accepted. Orphan permission/review/audit
rows, partial/duplicate/conflicting control rows and advanced state deny without
reset or repair. The singleton control and identical revision-one immutable
history bind the full manifest digest and owner; they are the genesis audit/
receipt, not a claim approval or reviewer assignment.

The fixed global native slot is independent of owner, audience, manifest, time
and manifest format version; no alternate/retry slot is exposed. This reservation
is required because SQL emptiness checks alone cannot safely elect competing
first writers. A future executor must check and reconcile the exact existing
native job before any insertion; UNKNOWN, conflict, or a failed job never
implicitly permits another submission. Controlled retry after a known rollback
is not implemented by this one-slot slice. SQL checks all 13 tables again inside
the transaction, inserts only control and control history, accepts only exact complete
REUSED state and rolls back/rethrows on errors. No grant, review, permission or

payloads (256 KiB) and complete revision histories (at most 64) fail closed;
decoding never infers approval from text or coerces bools into revisions.

Supplied-history validation requires revision one through the exact current
head without gaps/duplicates, correct parent-record hashes, monotonic recorded
time and immutable SKU/formula identity for claim/evidence chains. Trusted
minimum-revision/checkpoint pins reject stale or conflicting supplied state.
Revocation is terminal within an identity chain; draft/revoked/expired/future
records cannot establish current permission. Independent current evidence-review
states must exactly cover and match every embedded claim evidence pin; revoked
or changed evidence denies use even if the old claim approval remains present.
This is not a native reader/writer, history authenticity proof, independent
study review, current access check or physical append-only enforcement.

**P6 authoritative DRAFT reader group — 2026-10-14:**
`src/application/campaign_policy_reader.py` preflights the two existing exact
BigQuery schemas and uses a fresh cache-disabled, parameterized single-statement
UNION snapshot for the requested policy/version. It never reads the local policy
manifest as a serving fallback. Caller-supplied trusted specification/header
hashes pin the scope/configuration; canonical JSON bytes, exact field sets,
per-row hashes, rule identities/areas and the complete 47-rule set hash are
validated. Exactly one header and 47 unique rules are required; missing, duplicate,
partial, oversized/noncanonical or changed data denies. The query and result
iteration are bounded to 50 rows; overflow cannot masquerade as a complete set.
The reader re-fetches the original native job and correlates exact project,
region, ID, SQL, parameters/configuration and successful DONE status before
returning an immutable snapshot. Known DRAFT storage cannot become serving-ready
through a changed status/boolean; `require_serving_ready` always denies this
format. The snapshot is configuration evidence, not authenticated approval,
fresh user access, scientific support, record-history/revocation proof or a
response-release capability. Provider RPC/result waits are bounded individually,
not a deployed cumulative deadline guarantee.
