**Priority 6 ownership — clarified 2026-10-14:** accuracy, grounding, compliance
and usability are requirements for **every enabled interaction**, not solely
prompt approval. Priority 6 owns measurable acceptance criteria, implementation
and verification of per-response quality enforcement, and decision evidence under
the existing E1/E2/E4/E5/R1/G4 milestones. Reuse existing G1/G2/X1 contracts and
backend validation; this clarification adds no new priority or batch. These
requirements are planned work, not a claim that all enforcement is implemented.

Before releasing each response or publishing its artifacts, apply the relevant
checks to the actual request, current eligible evidence and candidate output:

- **Accuracy and grounding:** factual wording and citations must match applicable
  evidence; distinguish supported facts, synthetic performance, styling-only
  references and proposed creative rationale. Interpretation and clarification
  must preserve user intent and avoid inventing missing preferences or facts.
- **Compliance:** enforce approved-claim scope, brand rules, versioned safety
  policy and output contracts. A prompt's approval or model confidence cannot
  authorize an unsupported benefit or replace current evidence checks.
- **Usability:** apply reviewed stage-specific criteria for clear, relevant
  responses, actionable clarification, valid campaign structure and readable,
  complete product/banner representation where artifacts are produced.
- **Failure handling:** insufficient evidence must lead to clarification, block
  or review as appropriate. Failed, unavailable or inconclusive required checks
  cannot silently pass or release a campaign as successful. Pending review is
  not approval; no automatic model replay is introduced.
- **Decision evidence:** retain a bounded versioned per-interaction decision with
  applied checks, applicability, outcomes, reasons and request/output/evidence/
  model/prompt/policy references in BigQuery. Use hashes/references and safe
  diagnostics; content logging remains subject to the existing privacy controls.

Priority 6 completion requires both measured benchmark results against reviewed
thresholds **and verified runtime release gates for every enabled interaction**.
Test valid, ambiguous, missing/stale evidence, unsupported claims, adversarial,
checker-failure and multi-turn cases, including denial before response/artifact
release. Disabled optional workflows must pass these same gates before activation.
Priority 9 UI/refinement wiring and Priority 12 shared deployment must carry and
verify these checks; they do not defer Priority 6's enforcement responsibility.
Measured quality and calibrated rubrics do not guarantee every future response
is correct; uncertainties and coverage limits must remain explicit.

- [x] (High; Must have — every enabled interaction/shared pilot) E1: Define reviewed, versioned accuracy, grounding, compliance and usability criteria for interpretation, clarification, campaign generation and enabled refinements. Create a small benchmark across representative SKUs, objectives and channels with expected evidence, outcomes and multi-turn cases. Include ambiguous requests, missing/stale evidence, pending/rejected claims, styling-only references, synthetic performance and injection attempts.
- [ ] (High evaluation; Critical runtime enforcement; Must have — every enabled interaction/shared pilot) E2: Build a repeatable runner with retrieval recall/precision at the configured top K where labeled evidence applies, citation validity, schema compliance, unsupported-claim checks, grounding correctness and stage-specific usability checks. Test exact SKU readers separately from semantic ranking. Implement and verify corresponding per-response release gates against actual current inputs and candidate outputs, including failed/unavailable/inconclusive checker handling and clarification/block/review outcomes; prior benchmark or prompt approval never bypasses these gates.
- [ ] (Medium; Good to have — managed scoring; baseline quality gates remain Must have) E3: Add supported managed groundedness/quality scoring with task-specific rubrics. Calibrate model-based scores against human review; retain deterministic checks as enforcement.
- [ ] (High; Must have — every enabled interaction/shared pilot; expanded production gates later) E4: Store evaluation inputs, expected evidence, per-case results, aggregate scores, failures and model/prompt/policy versions in BigQuery. Also persist bounded versioned per-interaction quality decisions, applied checks/applicability, outcomes, reason codes and evidence/output references under existing privacy controls. Set reviewed thresholds for runtime acceptance and promotion, and add regression checks for prompt/model/policy/retrieval and enabled-workflow changes.
- [ ] (High evaluation; Critical artifact release; Must have — composition pilot; generative checks before enabling that feature) E5: Evaluate banner readability, full product representation and copy compliance, and enforce the applicable validated criteria before each artifact is published/released. Review and gate image fidelity separately before generative rendering is enabled.

### Observability

- [ ] (High; Must have — shared pilot) O1: Emit structured stage events for interpretation, retrieval, original-image verification, model calls, validation, rendering, registration and review. Capture duration, retries, failure category, model usage, evidence IDs and BigQuery job IDs.
- [ ] (Medium; Good to have — pilot tracing; High Must have for broader production) O2: Instrument custom workflow stages with OpenTelemetry and export to Cloud Trace. Route correlated operational logs to Cloud Logging and use managed model metrics where available.
- [ ] (High; Must have — pilot essential alerts; richer dashboards optional) O3: Create dashboards for successful/blocked/failed requests, stage latency, retries, token usage, grounding/schema failures and cost estimates. Configure actionable alerts with thresholds appropriate to POC traffic.
- [ ] (Critical; Must have — before logging content / shared pilot) O4: Define redaction, sampling, access and retention before capturing prompt/response content. Record artifact references rather than large image payloads in operational logs.

Agent Engine observability applies if that runtime is selected. Calling Gemini from the existing Python workflow alone does not trace all application stages. Verify any managed request-response logging configuration before relying on it.

### Responsible AI and guardrails

- [ ] (Critical; Must have — shared pilot) G1: Define a versioned policy with allow, block and needs-review outcomes for unsupported benefits, ingredient concentrations, medical/outcome promises, offers, fabricated metrics and out-of-scope requests. Preserve existing strict output checks.
- [ ] (Critical; Must have — shared pilot) G2: Complete policy-integrated safety reporting. Explicit settings and prompt/candidate safety/finish checks are implemented for both interpretation and planning as part of M1; blocked/incomplete JSON cannot produce a preview. Versioned verdict records, UI explanations and broader policy integration remain pending.
- [ ] (Critical; Must have — shared-pilot coverage/enforcement; named provider optional) G3: Evaluate and integrate appropriate input/output screening for injection, harmful content, PII and secrets. Verify Model Armor region, language and image support for asia-south1; document any coverage gaps and detector failures.
- [ ] (Critical; Must have — pilot adversarial baseline; broader evaluation before production) G4: Add adversarial and Responsible AI evaluation for injection, misleading beauty promises, discriminatory/body-shaming copy and relevant skin-tone representation. Test false positives as well as missed violations.
- [ ] (Critical; Must have — shared pilot; Workspace activation deferred) G5: Complete Workspace SSO and server-side role/ownership enforcement through A1–A5. Prevent cross-user access and define privacy/retention behavior. Keep creative review, benefit-claim approval and launch authority separate.

Prompt instructions and managed safety filters supplement code-enforced source eligibility and claim validation. Passing a content filter does not approve a skincare claim.

### Explainability

- [ ] (High; Must have — shared pilot) X1: Store guardrail decision records containing rule ID, policy version, outcome, reason code and relevant evidence/review references.
- [ ] (High; Must have — shared pilot) X2: Show which evidence supplied product wording, which rules constrained the output, which sources were styling-only, and why a request was blocked or needs review.
- [ ] (Low; Good to have — rank/distance display; eligibility enforcement remains Critical Must have) X3: Show retrieval scope, eligibility checks and ranking signals. Present cosine distance as similarity, with no performance or confidence claim attached.
- [ ] (High; Must have — shared pilot) X4: Label model-generated creative rationale as a proposal. Support explanations with recorded evidence and decisions; do not claim to expose internal model reasoning.

### Token and compute cost management

- [ ] (High; Must have — shared pilot) C1: Capture per-attempt model usage, including input, output, cached and thinking tokens where reported, embedding calls/reuse, retries, latency and missing-usage states. Track evaluation and guardrail service usage too.
- [ ] (High; Must have — shared pilot) C2: Record BigQuery job IDs, billed/processed bytes, slot time and cache hits where available. Apply query byte limits consistently and account for the project's pricing model.
- [ ] (High; Must have — pilot estimates; billing reconciliation before production) C3: Build estimated request costs from versioned pricing, currency and effective dates. Keep estimates distinct from billed costs; missing usage must not appear as zero cost. Reconcile aggregate estimates against Cloud Billing exports in BigQuery.
- [ ] (Critical; Must have — shared pilot) C4: Add configurable input/output limits, maximum model calls and retries, request deadlines, concurrency limits and daily/request budget admission checks per verified principal/workspace and allowed model. Reserve estimated budget for in-flight requests to reduce concurrent overspend; account for failures and completed billable work.
- [ ] (High; Must have — shared pilot) C5: Track rendering/workflow duration now. Configure CPU/memory, maximum instances, concurrency and timeout controls when choosing a deployed runtime. Treat per-request allocation of shared compute as an estimate.
- [ ] (High; Must have — pilot spend/alerts and production reconciliation; detailed reporting optional) C6: Report spend by verified principal/workspace, model, stage, SKU and request, including embeddings, BigQuery, compute, GCS, network, telemetry, evaluations and screening. Add budget alerts and verify any service-specific spend-cap support; standard alerts alone do not enforce a spending limit.

Reuse authoritative embedding records when source identity, hash, model and dimensions match. Any future context caching must preserve current evidence and approval checks. Synthetic campaign advertising budgets are separate from application operating costs.

## Completion gates

Local UI development can proceed with the existing bounded generator. A shared POC demo needs A1–A5 Workspace authentication/authorization, ownership-scoped persistence/downloads, working request validation, grounding failure handling, basic safety verdicts, authorized review, usage tracking, bounded retries/concurrency and clear POC labeling. Any exposed model choices must complete M1–M4; model migration is required before the configured model retires.

Before broader production use, complete the quality benchmark and agreed acceptance thresholds, versioned prompt/policy promotion, privacy controls, centralized dashboards/alerts, billing reconciliation and enforced application spending controls. Replace POC claim fixtures with real approved evidence before authorizing benefits. Real campaign delivery requires its own authorization and outcome measurement.

## Working sequence

Use the [severity, necessity and delivery lanes](#severity-necessity-and-delivery-lanes) to schedule the existing [priority IDs](#implementation-priority-order); Critical/High Must have work precedes optional work, subject to dependencies. Workspace/membership/conversation/attempt/stage-event/model-invocation/evidence/audit contract foundations are complete; the sharing coordinator contract is complete offline. The sharing transaction SQL/query contract is also complete offline. The disabled-by-default sharing executor/sealed-intent foundation is complete with offline tests. Isolated native SQL/SDK/race checks are complete. Read-only authoritative receipt recovery and the offline typed retry/seal contract are complete. Trusted retry executor integration is complete offline. Isolated native retry verification is complete. The signing-key/version retention contract is complete offline. The pinned secret-loader foundation is complete offline. The publication/refresh contract and all fixed Priority 4 milestones are complete within their documented default-disabled implementation and verification scopes. Continue with Priority 5's versioned prompt library and core guardrails. Company SSO activation remains deferred. Complete the shared-pilot and production gates before advancing their release scope. The detailed A/U/F/M/P/E/R/O/G/X/C checklists describe the work within those priorities rather than a competing sequence.

Update statuses and validation evidence after each completed milestone. Tag each change with severity, necessity and release scenario; identify the next existing ID in the active delivery lane. Explain the scoped implementation before modifying code, preserve existing data and approvals, and avoid marking planned integrations as implemented.

**Critical / Must have — shared pilot, existing P6 E2 scope.** Isolated native

`campaign_planner_projection.py` implements `campaign-planner-copy-v1`: duplicate
JSON keys, extra/missing fields and values outside the supplied enum options deny.

fixed order. Only permitted null body maps to the explicit empty option.

exact ResolvedPrompt and fresh authority capture. Ordered supplied raw turns must
match every stored transcript hash. It pins evidence/options, invocation binding,
rendered text and image asset/generation/MIME/size/byte hashes. Its diagnostic
outcome bridge validates the durable successful provider response and projects copy
against the prepared options. No raw transcript is stored in the manifest.

## Durable planner inputs and retained transcript bodies — 2026-10-16

**Critical / Must have — existing P6 E2 execution binding.** The opt-in
`ProtectedGenerationAdmission` composition accepts a server-installed
`planner_input_builder`. It inserts `invocation_input_manifests` within the same
transaction as prompt binding, invocation/attempt dispatch claim, audit and workspace

changed server-held envelope. SQL checks current immutable selection, binding and

single provider callback; recovery never dispatches. The legacy callback and catalog
defaults remain unchanged. The new callback receives `(profile, resolved, prepared)`;
installation is explicit and default serving remains disabled.

`run_authoritative_quality(..., prepared_inputs=...)` now supports the versioned
structured projector. It rereads the exact stored manifest before/after checking,
compares selected authority/catalog/transcript pins, verifies the durable successful
response bytes and constrains candidate text AND sections to the projected copy.
Current access, policy, prompt, product and independent claim/evidence checks still
surround execution. Input/projection digests enter the context record. These guards
do not implement the remaining semantic, visual or channel deliverable checks and
never grant release on unavailable checks.

The user selected **30-day raw chat retention**. The separate
`campaign_transcript_bodies` table partitions on recorded_at with 30-day partition
expiration and mandatory partition filters. Readers enforce the exact rolling
30-day cutoff as well. Opt-in authority publication accepts `transcript_texts` and
writes the exact hash-matched ordered batch atomically with the authority row;
replays require the same complete retained batch. The existing global job slot is
preserved: a prior hash-only publication cannot silently acquire raw bodies through
an alternate slot. Retention expiry withholds transcript use, rather than fabricating
missing messages. Native partition expiration is asynchronous and partition-based;
application eligibility uses exact timestamps. No raw text enters diagnostic files.
The raw bodies remain private application records, not prompt instructions.

`prepare_from_stored_transcript` composes fresh owner-authorized authority reads,
stored-body verification and immutable planner-input preparation. It never fills
missing turns from local/browser history. Unpersisted chat remains unsupported.
The original server-held prepared envelope is still needed for dispatch/outcome
verification; cold reconstruction is not added. Source evidence/image authorization
still belongs to trusted source adapters; hashes alone do not authenticate provenance
or prove a provider consumed the bytes. Raw retained records require the protected
writer/reader paths; no public transcript endpoint is installed.

### Trusted operator-policy publication and revocation — 2026-10-17

Critical / Must have, existing fixed MVP operational-approval prerequisites.
Implemented campaign_operator_policy_publication.py and the disabled-by-default
owned-process campaign_operator_policy_publisher.py in successive small groups.
Publication requires authenticated IAP plus an explicit server-owned publisher
manifest (at most 300 seconds). ADC/application ADMIN never creates publisher or
reviewer authority. No production manifest, operator assignment or serving control
bootstrap was configured. This infrastructure governs reviewer management; it is
not acceptance/clarification policy approval or claim permission.

The publisher captures the complete operator head/history and permission-control
head/history in one bounded native-correlated snapshot, validates exact table
schemas, and checks identity/manifest before and after reads/writes/recovery.
Genesis/replacement and terminal revocation check exact source prefixes inside
the transaction, share the unpartitioned permission-control fence with existing
review/permission writers, and atomically write head/history/control/audit. Audit
actions use a distinct OPERATOR_POLICY namespace; manifest references are not
reviewer grants. Revocation preserves policy content and remains possible after
the policy's effective window expires, under a current explicit publisher manifest.
Current reads reject revoked or expired operator policies.

There is one stable production native slot per policy revision, independent of
publisher, timestamps and payload. Repeated preparation of the same logical
operation returns the retained intent; changed content conflicts. Intents are
server-held, never HTTP-deserialized. Dispatch consumes the intent before I/O;
uncertainty recovers only the original job. Foreign/malformed receipts remain
UNKNOWN. Cold recovery, expired-manifest recovery and retry/advance after a failed
reserved slot are withheld; this API supplies no alternate or fail-forward slot.
These limits are explicit and do not grant permission to bypass the native slot.

All 115 focused publication/publisher/capture/storage/reader/management/dependency
tests pass; compilation and dependencies pass. No full suite was repeated.
The reusable five-table native probe passes all six final cases:
genesis, original-job recovery, same-slot/different-config denial, competing fence
writes, positive rollback, and revocation/current-read denial. Competition has one
COMMITTED and one positively NOT_COMMITTED outcome with native concurrent-update
evidence at the shared control row; overlap duration was not measured. Distinct
owned fixture slots exercise SQL contention separately from stable-slot collision.
Initial UNKNOWN observations recovered original jobs only, without resubmission.
Exact five-table snapshots match after forced rollback and slot denial.

`campaign_policy_approval_publication.py` defines four immutable transitions:
CREATE_AUTHORITY, CREATE_RECEIPT, REVOKE_AUTHORITY and REVOKE_RECEIPT. Complete
supplied source captures, the full shared permission-control chain, exact expected
source rows, a common unpartitioned control update and an atomic audit fence every
mutation. No source row is deleted or replaced. One stable native slot per target
and action is independent of actor, content and time; UNKNOWN or known rollback
cannot mint an alternate slot. Reuse is original native-job recovery, not SQL
resubmission. Revocation is terminal. These pure plans are disabled by default.

admission and subsequently recover only the original job. Missing, changed,
revoked, expired or foreign reviewer authority withholds the result. Current exact
postreads must match a successful business receipt; a commit without its verified
receipt/readback remains UNKNOWN. Cold/lost-session intent reconstruction and
expired publisher-manifest recovery are not supported.

outcome-publication plans are retained before writes for exact lost-response
recovery. Cold recovery reads original native jobs; no cold redispatch or new slot.

called against a live model in this slice.
